content: Use CreatePendingRemote for non-network factories on commit

This CL replaces CreateAndConnectToPendingReceiver with
CreatePendingRemote when wrapping scheme-specific non-network
subresource URLLoaderFactory instances in
RenderFrameHostImpl::CommitNavigation.

Previously, CommitNavigation allocated an intermediate PendingRemote and
called CreateAndConnectToPendingReceiver for each scheme-specific
non-network factory, which forced URLLoaderFactoryBuilder::Finish to
connect through an extra Mojo pipe when no intercepting proxies were
added. Using CreatePendingRemote returns the terminal PendingRemote
directly when the builder is empty.

Add SEQUENCE_CHECKER to NetTaskQueues

NetTaskQueues instances and their methods are expected to be accessed
from a single sequence (the network thread / associated sequence).
This CL adds SEQUENCE_CHECKER and DCHECK_CALLED_ON_VALID_SEQUENCE to
ensure sequence affinity.

content: Revert one-shot behavior in ScopedSelectFileDialogFactory

This is a partial revert of https://crrev.com/c/8528927
(commit a66a5557ded970b949dd741c010b226a61171426).

CL 8528927 made content::ScopedSelectFileDialogFactory consume its
configured result on the first dialog request (one-shot). However, per
the spec discussion in
https://github.com/w3c/webdriver-bidi/issues/1177, it was decided that
the configured file picker behavior should persist across subsequent
dialog requests until explicitly reconfigured or reset at the end of
the test/scope.

Revert the one-shot state consumption in
content::ScopedSelectFileDialogFactory by removing
OneShotSelectFileDialogFactory and passing FakeSelectFileDialogFactory
or CancellingSelectFileDialogFactory to
ui::ScopedSetSelectFileDialogFactory. Configured results now persist
for subsequent file dialog requests within the scope instead of being
consumed on first use. Document this lifetime, and that std::nullopt
cancels every request, in the ScopedSelectFileDialogFactory class
comment.

Also update the corresponding comments in web_test.mojom and
select_file_dialog_automation.test-mojom, and update
file_system_chooser_test_helpers_unittest.cc (renaming the one-shot
test to PersistsConfiguredFilesAcrossMultipleDialogs and using gMock
container matchers) while preserving the factory restoration behavior
and its unit tests.

Additionally, fix includes in file_system_chooser_test_helpers.{h,cc}
and its unit test: include base/check_op.h for CHECK_EQ, directly
include ui/shell_dialogs/selected_file_info.h in the header for
ui::SelectedFileInfo, and include base/memory/scoped_refptr.h in the
unit test for scoped_refptr.

ash: Use MyFiles under the user data dir in browser tests

On linux-chromeos (i.e. not running on a ChromeOS device), the primary
user's MyFiles directory is the default downloads directory
(DownloadPrefs::GetDefaultDownloadDirectory()), unless
--use-myfiles-in-user-data-dir-for-testing is specified.
InProcessBrowserTest overrides chrome::DIR_DEFAULT_DOWNLOADS with a temp
directory, so browser tests have relied on the override.

This CL makes InProcessBrowserTest always pass
--use-myfiles-in-user-data-dir-for-testing on ChromeOS, so MyFiles is
<user data dir>/<user dir>/MyFiles in browser tests. This is the same
layout as on ChromeOS devices, and it is still under the temporary user
data directory.

This is a preparation to compute the MyFiles directory for linux-chromeos
without depending on //chrome (DownloadPrefs), so that the User-based
file_manager::util path functions can be moved to
//chromeos/ash/components/file_manager.

HoldingSpaceKeyedServiceBrowserTest used to override
chrome::DIR_DEFAULT_DOWNLOADS to change MyFiles. It now uses the test
user's MyFiles directory directly.

[Glic] Track pending task for background continuations on Android

When a follow-up continuation request arrives on Android while Chrome is
in the background after the previous turn completed, no active actor
task or pending entry holds the foreground service during the model
round-trip before the next task starts.

This change:
1. Adds a separately named actor service method that registers a pending
   task without notifying pending task added observers, so background
   continuations keep the foreground service alive without preparing an
   offscreen tab.
2. On Android, registers that pending entry and observes task creation
   when a background continuation arrives with a live Glic instance and
   no currently actuating task.
3. Clears the pending entry when the turn yields to the user or pauses
   (for example to ask a clarifying question) and on terminal task
   updates, in addition to task start, invoke errors, stop requests, and
   disconnect. Pending entries never expire, so a yield must clear them.
4. Adds unit tests covering registration, cleanup on task start,
   terminal completion, yield and pause, and the skip paths (already
   actuating, no instance).

Bug: 580708121

[Clank][ImageWriter] (3/5) Cache probed USB capacity per attached drive

The Recovery Utility extension calls listRemovableStorageDevices every
2 seconds while its drive picker is open. On Android, Chrome usually
cannot read a USB drive's size from sysfs (SELinux), so every call
asked each such drive directly: open it, claim its interface (which
detaches the kernel's usb-storage driver), send SCSI TEST UNIT READY
and READ CAPACITY (10), then release the interface (which re-attaches
usb-storage). That removed and re-created the drive's block device
every 2 seconds for as long as the picker was open.

Remember each successful probe result in a process-global
AndroidUsbStorageProvider singleton (initialized and torn down by
ChromeBrowserMainPartsAndroid, matching ChromeOS
ash::disks::DiskMountManager), keyed by the device's GUID (which
UsbDevice assigns as a fresh v4 UUID per physical connection), so each
drive is probed once per plug-in instead of on every poll. Concurrent
GetAllDevices calls share the in-flight enumeration pass rather than
opening the USB device twice, and cross-platform
RemovableStorageProvider::GetAllDevices remains unchanged.

Entries are dropped when:
- The drive is unplugged. It disappears from GetDevices(), and a
  replugged drive gets a new GUID.
- Chrome can no longer read the device (for example, its USB
  permission was revoked).
Zero or failed probes are never cached (and evict any prior entry), so a
drive that is still spinning up or a card reader whose card was just
inserted appears on the next 2-second poll rather than staying hidden
behind a negative cache.

Known limit, by design: a USB card reader that stays plugged in while
its card is swapped or removed keeps showing the old capacity until the
reader is unplugged. This is safe because every write re-reads the
capacity (READ CAPACITY (10) in MojoUsbBotSession) and refuses images
that do not fit, and a missing card fails at TEST UNIT READY. A
time-based expiry was left out on purpose: each re-probe would detach
usb-storage again, which is exactly the churn this change removes.

Test: unit_tests --gtest_filter=*RemovableStorageProviderAndroidTest*

Revert absolute path CHECK in ShouldBlockAccessToPath to DCHECK

Revert CHECK(path.IsAbsolute()) in ShouldBlockAccessToPath back to
DCHECK(path.IsAbsolute()) with a CHECK-exclusion TODO. When a non-
absolute FilePath is passed, the non-fatal CHECK triggers a crash or
dump. Converting this check back to DCHECK avoids crashes when non-
absolute paths are passed.

This fix was generated by the Crash Autofix pipeline for crash report
crash/8283db8088400000. Please see go/crash-autofix for more details.

ebf945a39e5201a3d34f71184f6e8ff7ee98abac
parent
919f575076b6519fc14c4789ca74ca240c19428c [ diff ] 
Revert "[X11] Don't set _GTK_FRAME_EXTENTS when the WM can't handle it"

This reverts commit 1aeb85d276b595cd54a07d497516769f6797fddf.

Reason for revert: ozone_x11_unittests failure

Failure Link: https://ci.chromium.org/ui/p/chromium/builders/ci/Linux%20Tests/190074/overview

Original change's description:
> [X11] Don't set _GTK_FRAME_EXTENTS when the WM can't handle it
>
> Since [1], X11Window::Map() calls UpdateDecorationInsets()
> unconditionally, so _GTK_FRAME_EXTENTS is set to the 4px resize
> border even on window managers for which CanSetDecorationInsets()
> returns false.  On Xfwm4 4.16 and older (e.g. Debian bullseye and
> Ubuntu 22.04), which mishandle removal of _GTK_FRAME_EXTENTS,
> maximized windows then end up 4px larger than the screen on each
> side (x=-4, width 1288 on a 1280px screen).  The fullscreen and
> tiled-state paths had the same problem.
>
> Move the CanSetDecorationInsets() check into UpdateDecorationInsets()
> so that every caller respects it.
>
> [1] https://crrev.com/c/5640510
>
> Fixed: 396808830
> Change-Id: Ie41d680105a6f71b43a4c14259587024fce6f243
> Reviewed-on: https://chromium-review.googlesource.com/c/chromium/src/+/8763710
> Reviewed-by: Nick Yamane <nickdiego@igalia.com>
> Commit-Queue: Thomas Anderson <thomasanderson@chromium.org>
> Cr-Commit-Position: refs/heads/main@{#1740083}

tree
efd4e5b2bf2d76811b91e6e43cea9a3a80a3bde2
parent
074c243ea63bb3021e825e25c91fb27ed8fbd83e [ diff ] 
[ash] System menu: Drop redundant AccountId check for teleport menu

SystemMenuModelBuilder::AppendTeleportMenu() looked up the AccountId
of the browser's profile via multi_user_util::GetAccountIdFromProfile()
only to check that it is valid, in addition to checking that the
browser window has a valid owner in ash::MultiUserWindowManager.

The owner of a browser window is set only by
MultiUserWindowManagerBrowserAdaptor::OnBrowserCreated(), and only when
the browser's AccountId is valid. So a valid window owner already
implies a valid AccountId for the browser, and the extra check is
redundant. Remove it, and the dependency on multi_user_util, which is
being removed.

Revert CHECK_EQ to DCHECK_EQ in background tab loading policy

Convert the CHECK_EQ on the primary main frame URL back to DCHECK_EQ in
ScheduleLoadForRestoredTabs. The check can fail during tab restoration
when the primary main frame's last committed URL is non-empty, causing
fatal check failures in release builds.

5fa478d49e780df22e9c52bff1c7d1353d7e35e3
parent
6bffd703e7c7f5f43ac52d74febe87fa9515d7c7 [ diff ] 
[ash] SigninErrorNotifier: Keep AccountId instead of re-resolving it

SigninErrorNotifier called multi_user_util::GetAccountIdFromProfile()
in five places to get the AccountId of the user owning the profile,
even though the constructor already resolves the user to compute the
user-scoped notification IDs.

Resolve the AccountId once in the constructor and keep it in a new
`account_id_` member, and use it everywhere. The anonymous
SaveForceOnlineSignin(Profile*) helper, which only existed to map the
profile to the AccountId, is inlined into its single caller.

This removes the last use of multi_user_util in
//chrome/browser/ash/login/signin, so drop the corresponding BUILD.gn
dependency and DEPS rule. multi_user_util is being removed.

when result.status was not PermissionStatus::GRANTED for one-time
session model settings.

[ash] Files: Use User's AccountId in GetLoggedInProfileInfoList

GetLoggedInProfileInfoList() already resolves the user_manager::User
for each loaded profile, but then calls
multi_user_util::GetAccountIdFromProfile() to look up the same user
again only to get its email.

removes one of the uses of multi_user_util, which is being removed.
The other use in this file (currentProfileId / displayedProfileId)
will be handled separately.

Cancel running jobs in SchedulingEmbedder
📋 Metadata

396f6913cbed0717752f53b743c98fa3d8e5bd65

tree
3d6e536ed90ea848beea14a95a41bd4a242874f7
parent
5902065d990a974f21092e925784b5019464ead7 [ diff ] 
Cancel running jobs in SchedulingEmbedder

Destroying an Embedder::Job handle only cancels a job that is still
queued. A job with passages in the in-flight batch goes back to the
queue when the batch returns and embeds all its remaining passages,
although nobody waits for the result, for example a Semantic Embedder
request from a page that went away.

This CL makes five changes:

1. Drop a running job in TryCancel() the same way as a queued one. Its
   callback is posted with kCanceled and its status is recorded right
   away.
2. Record which job each part of a batch comes from, and skip the
   results of jobs that are gone when the batch returns.
3. Use those parts to find the job that owns a vector that can't be
   normalized, instead of failing the first unfinished job. No job
   fails if the owner was canceled or the vector comes after the last
   passage. A batch that returns nothing fails its first job unless
   that job was canceled.
4. Say in the Embedder::TryCancel() comment that a job can be canceled
   until it finishes, and what its callback then gets.
5. Keep the job handles in tests that expect a running job to finish
   normally. Add tests for canceling running jobs, and for which job
   fails on a vector that can't be normalized.

tree
a2ff69bf9ae0e4ec6d48b63f35dbfe79ac313016
parent
61a8bc37cfb469e3b5b03059c2d46b2428312634 [ diff ] 
[ash] multi_user: drop unused multi_user_util pieces

GetProfileFromWindow() has no callers, and GetAccountIdFromEmail() was
only used by multi_user_util_chromeos_unittest.cc. That test checked
that GetAccountIdFromProfile() keeps working after the refresh token is
revoked, which dates back to when the AccountId came from the identity
manager; the implementation no longer looks at the identity manager, so
the test has nothing left to cover. Remove all three.

Four tests included multi_user_util.h without using it. Drop the
includes and the //chrome/browser/ui/ash/multi_user deps their targets
held only for that.

Also drop the //chrome/browser/ash/arc/boot_phase_monitor dep and
circular-include allowance from //chrome/browser/ui/ash/multi_user,
left over since the boot phase monitor stopped including
multi_user_util.h, and the signin DEPS allowances only the removed test
used.

This is a step towards removing multi_user_util.

DisableCustomMediaKeyHandler crashed when called with a delegate that
did not match the registered delegate for the context. This reverts the

🌺 Revert "cc: Notify ContextCacheController when TryFlushPendingWork flushes"

This reverts commit 97825515be2202a0c85e94ea4e719a3bedca6ebc.

Reason for revert: GpuImageDecodeCachePurgeOnTimerTest#PurgeNotifiesCacheControllerAndFreesResourcesOnIdle/GpuImageDecodeCacheTests.* in cc_unittests is deterministically failing (6/6 runs across 5 alerting Android CI builders: android-14-arm64-rel, android-12l-x64-dbg-tests, android-16-x64-dbg-tests, android-11-x86-rel, and android-13-x64-rel) since this change landed at r1739952.

Example failing build: https://ci.chromium.org/ui/p/chromium/builders/ci/android-14-arm64-rel/21026

The new PurgeNotifiesCacheControllerAndFreesResourcesOnIdle test and SetCacheControllerTaskRunnerForTesting(task_runner_) wiring cause cc_unittests on Android to crash with [FATAL:base/synchronization/lock.cc:114] DCHECK failed: owning_thread_ref_ == PlatformThread::CurrentRef() because ContextCacheController asserts the context lock is held when invoked.

Please feel free to re-land once the context lock acquisition in GpuImageDecodeCachePurgeOnTimerTest / TryFlushPendingWork is addressed.

Original change's description:
> cc: Notify ContextCacheController when TryFlushPendingWork flushes
>
> This CL updates GpuImageDecodeCache::TryFlushPendingWork to return early
> when there are no pending unlock or deletion operations, and to notify
> ContextCacheController via ClientBecameBusy and ClientBecameNotBusy
> around flushing pending context work.
>
> This fixes a 1 MiB GPU memory regression exposed by
> PrioritizeCompositorTaskRunnerOnStartup (crrev.com/c/8741842):
> prioritizing ui::Compositor during startup causes the browser UI
> compositor to draw an intermediate frame before settling, uploading a
> transient image that subsequent frames no longer reference. After the
> worker context goes idle and releases its 1 MiB command buffer ring
> buffer, the 30-second purge timer evicts that entry and emits a
> DeleteTransferCacheEntryINTERNAL command. Because TryFlushPendingWork
> acquired the context lock directly without notifying
> ContextCacheController, the 1-second idle cleanup callback never ran,
> leaving the re-allocated 1 MiB ring buffer allocated indefinitely.
>
> Returning early when no unlock or deletion operations are pending avoids
> issuing redundant shallow flushes and prevents no-op ReduceCacheUsage or
> purge calls from triggering needless ContextCacheController busy-to-idle
> cleanup wakeups.
>
> Verified on Pinpoint (system_health.memory_desktop /
> browse:news:hackernews:2020 on win-arm64-snapdragon-elite-perf, N=20):
> gpu:effective_size median dropped from 10,458,416 B to 9,465,394 B
> (-993,022 B / -9.49%).
> https://pinpoint-dot-chromeperf.appspot.com/job/11245dff990000
>
> Bug: 582565671

Fix renderer main thread priority on Linux

This patch makes renderer main thread get the matching priority, nice
level -8, for its thread type kPresentation on Linux.

Chromium gives a set of threads higher priority when the browser process
is running with the capability CAP_SYS_NICE. One such thread is the
renderer main thread. In case CAP_SYS_NICE is not given to the process,
it silently fails (there is a VLOG(1)).

The SandboxedProcessThreadTypeHandler (a base::ThreadTypeDelegate),
which forwards thread type changes to the browser from child processes,
is in the renderer process registered after its
blink::scheduler::WebThreadScheduler is created. WebThreadScheduler
instantiates a MainThreadSchedulerImpl which requests an update to the
thread type in its constructor via MaybeUpdateThreadTypeLease.

In case there is no registered base::ThreadTypeDelegate, base::Thread
applies the thread type (i.e nice priority for Linux) directly which
fails in the renderer. This makes the renderer main thread stay at nice
0 even when the browser has CAP_SYS_NICE and could raise its priority.

As the SandboxedProcessThreadTypeHandler needs to have a task runner in
order to bind it's mojo interface, and no task runner exists until the
WebThreadScheduler has been instaitated there is a ordering dep-cycle
to get the renderer process up and running.

To fix this problem, this patch makes SandboxedProcessThreadTypeHandler
not bind thread_type_switcher_ until OnSwitcherConnected() is called,
which removes the dependency on the task runner being available during
construction. This allows us to construct the
SandboxedProcessThreadTypeHandler before the WebThreadScheduler is
created and hence the delegate can pass thread type to the browser
process for the renderer main thread.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2df725bc9b6b44620484e245942347a0e9f9cd77 [ diff ] 
[Capture] Fix and re-enable AuraWindowVideoCaptureDevice browser tests

The AuraWindowVideoCaptureDeviceBrowserTest suite has been disabled
since 2020 for flakiness. Re-enabling the suite exposed three issues:

1. WaitForFrameWithColor() checks capture_stack()->Started() before
   waiting for a frame to arrive. FrameSinkVideoCaptureDevice only
   calls OnStarted() when delivering its first frame, so checking
   Started() before HasCapturedFrames() races with first-frame
   delivery. Move the Started() check inside the HasCapturedFrames()
   loop, matching https://crrev.com/c/8500761.

2. On ChromeOS, content_shell places shell()->window() inside a shared
   WMTestHelper root window. Capturing the root window instead of
   shell()->window() places ScopedForceVisible on the root window, so
   an occluding sibling window in the root window still occludes the
   WebContents view. Restore GetCapturedWindow() to return
   shell()->window() on ChromeOS and verify that CapturesOccludedWindows
   continues capturing content changes while occluded.

3. In FrameSinkVideoCapturerImpl::MaybeDeliverFrame(), failed captures
   (such as when a non-root SubtreeCaptureId copy request returns an
   empty result because a new CompositorFrame activates before draw)
   skipped calling oracle_->CompleteCapture(). This left the oracle's
   pending frame count permanently non-zero, causing the oracle to
   reject all subsequent passive refresh requests. Always call
   oracle_->CompleteCapture() when a capture finishes.

Based on https://crrev.com/c/3522803 by mfoltz@.

Bug: 216815984
Test: content_browsertests, viz_unittests

85df5b18c96b6140a01dc78fc46b04caca73787c [ diff ] 
Revert "[webium] Don't defer layout in a tab drag window"

This reverts commit 970c7b3213238c712394ab9086b0e286e105fe9d.

Reason for revert: Failures in `TabDragging/DetachToBrowserTabDragControllerTest.OffsetForDraggingDetachedTab`

Failure Link: https://ci.chromium.org/ui/p/chromium/builders/ci/linux-wayland-weston-rel-tests/21561/test-results

Original change's description:
> [webium] Don't defer layout in a tab drag window
>
> http://go/scrcast/NjcwNTMyNDUwMDE4OTE4NHwzOWM5YjQzOC02Yg
>
> DeferLayoutDuringBrowserStartup skips BrowserView layout while the
> window is hidden and its size is unchanged. A tab torn off into a new
> window is attached while that window is still hidden, so the layout
> after the attach is skipped and the tab strip keeps its empty-window
> position. TabDragController computes the drag offset from that stale
> position, and the torn-off window stays right of the cursor for the
> whole drag.
>
> This CL turns the deferral off for windows created for a tab drag, as
> it already is for non-normal windows. TabDragController creates such a
> window hidden, attaches the tabs, places the window from its tab
> strip, and then shows it. Startup windows still defer; a torn-off
> window lays out as it does with the feature off.
>
> This CL also re-enables OffsetForDraggingDetachedTab on Linux, Mac and
> ChromeOS, so the tear-off offset has end-to-end coverage. Its Linux
> flakiness came from local displays without a window manager; it is
> stable on the bots. Windows stays disabled for a separate offset error
> from the window frame border.
>
> Bug: b:582604270, b:525236301
> Test: browser_tests --gtest_filter=BrowserViewDeferLayoutTest.*
> Test: interactive_ui_tests --gtest_filter=*.OffsetForDraggingDetachedTab*
> Change-Id: I083ce75bb64c9a1a609b837c63befb866a6a6964
> Reviewed-on: https://chromium-review.googlesource.com/c/chromium/src/+/8750563
> Reviewed-by: Rakina Zata Amni <rakina@chromium.org>
> Reviewed-by: Thomas Lukaszewicz <tluk@chromium.org>
> Commit-Queue: Ming-Ying Chung <mych@chromium.org>
> Cr-Commit-Position: refs/heads/main@{#1740000}

Bug: b:582604270, b:525236301

Replace large cursor size with custom cursor size in CursorClient

their getters with SetCustomCursorSize(std::optional<int>) and

CursorClientObserver are updated in the same way, and
Shell::SetLargeCursorSizeInDip() is removed.

- AccessibilityController maps the large cursor prefs to the custom
  size and clamps the pref value (previously done in CursorManager).
- DockedMagnifierController uses kDefaultLargeCursorSize explicitly.
- MouseShakeToLocateController saves/restores a single value.

09434e35d0075e06ac324cc0d176ab36403288fb [ diff ] 
[ImageFetcher] Share downloaded image data

Downloaded image bytes are copied when passed through callbacks and
delivered to multiple subscribers. Keep the bytes in a reference-counted
buffer so subscribers can share ownership without copying the payload.

Expose RefCountedMemory so consumers can use encoded bytes independently
of their backing storage. Move network and cached responses into shared
buffers and update the Android, ChromeOS, and iOS consumers. Preserve
empty-result behavior and the copies required at platform, decoder, and
persistence boundaries. Add tests covering concurrent and late
subscribers, binary data, byte-backed storage, and buffer lifetime after
the fetcher is destroyed.

[Views] Convert unhandled scroll events to wheel events only once
📋 Metadata
commit
09434e35d0075e06ac324cc0d176ab36403288fb
[ log ]
author
Thomas Anderson <thomasanderson@chromium.org>
Tue Nov 10 05:43:42 2026
committer
Chromium LUCI CQ <chromium-scoped@luci-project-accounts.iam.gserviceaccount.com>
Tue Nov 10 05:43:42 2026
tree
ffbbb6ded8e0c80ce3945205a9cbdf33fc717d9d
parent
e5895e935b380c5bfe22722b1209f16a972d54b8 [ diff ] 
[Views] Convert unhandled scroll events to wheel events only once

On Desktop Aura, DesktopNativeWidgetAura::OnScrollEvent() forwarded
a ui::EventType::kScroll event to Widget::OnScrollEvent(), which
already converts unhandled scroll events into mouse wheel events but
never marked the original event as handled. DesktopNativeWidgetAura
then saw an unhandled event and converted it a second time, so every
unhandled touchpad scroll event reached views as two wheel events.

On Linux, BrowserRootView switches tabs on wheel scrolls over the tab
strip once the accumulated delta rounds to a whole wheel tick. The
doubled delta meant that a touchpad scroll of only ~0.25 tick, e.g.
from resting a finger on a clickpad's button area before clicking a
tab, switched to a different tab.

Remove the redundant conversion from DesktopNativeWidgetAura, and make
Widget::OnScrollEvent() mark the original scroll event as handled when
either the scroll event or the synthesized wheel event was handled, so
callers can tell the event was consumed.

Fixed: 40650182

433bfc5a1d5fd98b0a2331de438b5ef5f0bf7077
parent
bd0779baa537c3ec8044ba7e9acff69043f1734d [ diff ] 
[Glic][Android] Keep native FRE card above the nav bar in edge-to-edge

When the tab bottom sheet covers the bottom controls (TabBottomSheet
edge-to-edge with GlicNoWebview), it draws behind the navigation bar,
and the bottom sheet controller expects the content to handle the
bottom safe area. Without that, the Start chat button of the native FRE
card can sit under the gesture bar.

- WebViewResizingHelper already pushes the bottom safe area inset to
  the WebUI and the resizing placeholder when the insets, the keyboard
  or the sheet margin change. It now also sends that value to an
  optional observer.
- TabBottomSheetCoordinator connects the observer to a new
  TabBottomSheetContent#onBottomSafeAreaInsetChanged() hook.
- GlicBottomSheetContent keeps the last value and applies it as the
  bottom margin of the FRE card content.

Before: https://screenshot.googleplex.com/526sbyDFih5Dc3K
After: https://screenshot.googleplex.com/xubQT6rJU9yfUQx
Bug: b:578631314
Test: run_chrome_junit_tests -f "*TabBottomSheet*:*WebViewResizingHelper*"

9a922c6b9aece3874478cf2c4500b7ee82b5972b

tree
ac6c48bbfe723e4fce1f8beb4ec2e97ff865ce26
parent
d9a894c30ff86537d6684726e1fbb7e8e2a438db [ diff ] 
[ash] Files: Remove dead teleported-window alert

fileManagerPrivate.getProfiles() returned currentProfileId and
displayedProfileId so that the Files app could detect that its window
had been teleported to another user's desktop, and then show an
"opened on a different desktop" alert after launching a task.

displayedProfileId was computed from the extensions AppWindow that
hosts the caller. The Files app is now a System Web App and no
longer runs in a Chrome App window, so the AppWindow lookup always
returns null, displayedProfileId always equals currentProfileId, and
isTeleported() always resolves to false. The alert is unreachable.

Remove the two fields from the IDL and the C++ implementation, the
isTeleported() helper, showOpenInOtherDesktopAlert() and its
now-unused strings. The profiles list in the response is unchanged.

This also drops one more use of multi_user_util from
private_api_misc.cc.

📝 Commit Message (First Line)
settings: Resolve pending dependencies of a restored settings tab
📋 Metadata
commit
d9a894c30ff86537d6684726e1fbb7e8e2a438db
[ log ]
author
James Cook <jamescook@chromium.org>
Tue Nov 10 05:20:24 2026
committer
Chromium LUCI CQ <chromium-scoped@luci-project-accounts.iam.gserviceaccount.com>
Tue Nov 10 05:20:24 2026
tree
72da3ca5a793a884dd047fbd1c76a31c86d48cbd
parent
14ab4e38a855724ee10f16b1bb80dfa37fbd047e [ diff ] 
settings: Resolve pending dependencies of a restored settings tab

With settings in a tab, the SettingsHostFragment and its child fragments
survive an activity recreation such as a theme change. The
FragmentManager restores them inside the activity's super.onCreate(),
before it has a WindowAndroid, SnackbarManager or BottomSheetController
and before the tab's SettingsPage exists, so the host hands the restored
fragments a provisional FragmentDependencyProvider in onAttach() whose
suppliers for those are empty.

Once the new SettingsPage adopted the host it re-provided the real
dependencies, but by then the fragments had already run onCreate() and
passed the empty suppliers on, e.g. ManageSyncSettings to its sign-out
button and batch upload card. Those never resolved, so signing out from
a restored settings tab failed an assert (or got a null
SnackbarManager). Re-providing also tripped the assert in
ManageSyncSettings.setSnackbarManagerSupplier() that the fragment is
still INITIALIZED, which crashed the restore itself.

Give FragmentDependencyProvider a createPending() factory that owns the
empty suppliers and a populateFrom() that resolves them from the real
provider, so whatever a fragment captured early resolves to the same
values as the real ones, and adding a dependency only touches that
class. SettingsHostFragment now keeps the first provider it gets: the
real one only populates the pending one, and a new SettingsPage adopting
a live host for the same tab changes nothing, since the dependencies are
scoped to the activity. No fragment is given its dependencies twice, so
the INITIALIZED assert stands.

Bug: 579078839, 521895796
Test: ManageSyncSettingsTest, SettingsHostFragmentTest
Change-Id: I8091b648a2786a7971ca494c7775c5540a988cd7

Reland "Roll src/third_party/fontconfig/src/ d416ada7b..f7e719706 (17 commits)"

This is a reland of commit d70bdac5db28c33d5bbadccd3183f5edbeaac3de.

The original roll broke Linux component builds: Chrome crashed in the
system libfontconfig (FcPatternGetFTFace) once GTK laid out text with
pango/cairo. In component builds libthird_party_fontconfig.so exported
the standard Fc* names, so the system cairo and pango that GTK loads
bound most Fc* calls to the bundled fontconfig, but FcPatternGetFTFace
and the FcFreeType* functions (which the bundled library lacks) to the
system one. Upstream commit d959fbe6 in this roll stores raw pointers
in heap FcPattern objects, which released fontconfigs still decode as
offsets.

The reland keeps the standard Fc* names hidden in all builds. In
component builds, the bundled library exports every public function a
second time as chromium_Fc*, and code that depends on
//third_party/fontconfig calls those names through a generated
fcchromium_rename.h. update.sh now generates the extra aliases in
fcaliastail.h, the rename header, and its include in fontconfig.h.

Original change's description:
> Roll src/third_party/fontconfig/src/ d416ada7b..f7e719706 (17 commits)
>
> Picks up upstream fontconfig changes, including additional
> validation in FcCacheOffsetsValid.
>
> Adds the new upstream src/fcindex.c to BUILD.gn; src/fcdir.c now
> calls FcIndexerQueryAll() from it.
>
> https://chromium.googlesource.com/external/fontconfig.git/+log/d416ada7b20a..f7e719706f68

> Created with:

[X11] Let the window manager move windows during tab dragging
📋 Metadata

6e5ee2c357125163c957405967d425394257d0fb

tree
b098cf39bd26c9c52643f5bd05878ad78ce3cb34
parent
1aeb85d276b595cd54a07d497516769f6797fddf [ diff ] 
[X11] Let the window manager move windows during tab dragging

When a tab is dragged out of a window on X11, Chrome moves the dragged
window itself with ConfigureRequests. Mutter (GNOME) and Muffin
(Cinnamon) treat those as client-initiated moves and keep the window
fully inside the work area, so a large dragged window gets stuck at the
screen edge and can't be brought to a tab strip below it.

Moves that the window manager performs in response to
_NET_WM_MOVERESIZE count as user actions and aren't constrained, which
is also how dragging by the title bar already works. Use them for the
tab-dragging move loop on the window managers the 2016 attempt
(https://crrev.com/2410773002) allowlisted: Compiz, Metacity, Muffin and
Mutter. Chrome keeps following the drag through the window's
ConfigureNotify events. Since the window manager holds the pointer grab,
the end of the drag is detected with XInput2 raw button release events
on the root window, and ending the loop early (attaching the tabs to
another tab strip) sends _NET_WM_MOVERESIZE_CANCEL. Newly created
windows start the move once they are mapped, because window managers
ignore the request for windows they don't manage yet. The move is
anchored at the grabbed point of the window, so the window snaps under
the pointer even if its initial position was constrained.

The 2016 change was reverted for crbug.com/661248, a click on the custom
title bar starting a move. That came from its title bar event handling,
which this change doesn't touch.

kX11WmMoveLoop is a kill switch.

Tested on Xvfb + Mutter 47.3 --x11: a tab dragged out of a 1500x900
window on a 1600x1000 screen now follows the pointer partly offscreen
(window y=661 vs. stuck at y=125 with the feature disabled). Also
checked reattaching to another tab strip mid-drag, detaching again in
the same gesture, Escape, a quick release and later clicks.

e29d64ef706f76551face07f78fc6df93a095d79 [ diff ] 
[X11] Don't set _GTK_FRAME_EXTENTS when the WM can't handle it

Since [1], X11Window::Map() calls UpdateDecorationInsets()
unconditionally, so _GTK_FRAME_EXTENTS is set to the 4px resize
border even on window managers for which CanSetDecorationInsets()
returns false.  On Xfwm4 4.16 and older (e.g. Debian bullseye and
Ubuntu 22.04), which mishandle removal of _GTK_FRAME_EXTENTS,
maximized windows then end up 4px larger than the screen on each
side (x=-4, width 1288 on a 1280px screen).  The fullscreen and
tiled-state paths had the same problem.

Move the CanSetDecorationInsets() check into UpdateDecorationInsets()
so that every caller respects it.

[1] https://crrev.com/c/5640510

e29d64ef706f76551face07f78fc6df93a095d79

tree
4ee3e5e724ba283bbce9f13e3e4ab576af3eb4ac
parent
088d7a203b05803912c79c523cdf119f342e2c45 [ diff ] 
[X11] Keep window size when toggling the system title bar

Switching "Use system title bar and borders" on and off left the
window larger and moved on window managers that honor
_GTK_FRAME_EXTENTS, such as Mutter. X11Window::SetUseNativeFrame()
changed the Motif hints but never updated _GTK_FRAME_EXTENTS, so the
stale client-side shadow insets stayed set while the WM drew its own
frame. On the way back, Mutter kept the decorated frame rectangle and
added the shadow insets around it, growing the window by the size of
the title bar plus the shadows on every on/off cycle.

After the new frame view is created, DesktopWindowTreeHostLinux now
asks the X11 window to update _GTK_FRAME_EXTENTS and then updates the
frame hints (previously only done for browser windows). When the
insets change on a mapped window, the X window bounds are adjusted so
the area inside the client-side decorations keeps its position and
size: with the system title bar the client area matches the
previously visible window, and switching back restores the original
bounds.

Without client-side shadows (no _GTK_FRAME_EXTENTS support or no
compositing), the opaque frame border is part of the visible window,
so CalculateInsetsInDIP() now returns empty insets in that case
instead of the border.

Fixed: 394152435

[crubit] Set `enable_rust_api_from_cpp = true` by default in Chromium.

This CL makes `rust_api_from_cpp` (aka `rs_bindings_from_cc`) available
in all Chromium builds (`enable_rust && build_with_chromium`), so that
it can be used for experimentation (and also so that things that work
currently are covered by `base_unittests`).  It is not yet officially
supported.

`//url:url_rs_api` is not built in Windows component builds, until
Crubit supports `__declspec(dllimport)`.

This CL is intentionally small to make a potential revert easier.
Other changes (e.g. docs updates) are postponed until this CL sticks -
see https://crrev.com/c/8758231.

strides and spans. Fail if the destination is not 8-bit Y_U_V 4:2:0.
The SkYUVColorSpace and SkColorSpace are not yet sent to the GPU process
(this is a TODO), so this does not change behavior.

In RasterImplementation, compute the shared memory layout for the planes
in a loop, in anticipation of supporting more than I420. On all
early-out paths, post `release_mailbox` and `readback_done` (with
failure), rather than dropping them.

Callers use media::VideoFrame::GetWritableVisibleSkYUVAPixmaps, and
pass the destination frame's color space as the SkColorSpace.

5e0d707deff17ed79f0da0d69ee71bf7f8a8c21b [ diff ] 
[Cast Remoting] Keep mirroring active until remoting negotiation succeeds

Previously, OpenscreenSessionHost::RequestRemotingStreaming called
StopStreaming and set state_ to State::kRemoting before sending the
remoting OFFER. This tore down the active mirroring audio and video
senders and paused capture before the receiver replied, causing an
interruption if the remoting OFFER timed out or was rejected.

Track the in-flight OFFER mode via PendingOfferMode and defer
StopStreaming and the transition to State::kRemoting until
OpenscreenSessionHost::OnNegotiated succeeds. If remoting negotiation
fails before OnNegotiated swaps the streams, state_ remains
State::kMirroring and RestartMirroringStreaming keeps the active
mirroring senders running without sending a redundant OFFER.

Bug: 379361280
Test: mirroring_unittests --gtest_filter=OpenscreenSessionHostTest.*

[🌷] Deflake BottomSheetTest#testAdditionalBottomOffset

Opening the bottom sheet pins the edge-to-edge bottom chin in
BottomControlsStacker, triggering an asynchronous renderer update to
the bottom controls min-height offset that calls
BottomSheetControllerImpl#setBottomControlsOffset and resets the
sheet container's bottom margin to 0. Wait for the bottom controls
min-height offset via waitForBottomControlsMinHeightOffset before
setting the test bottom margin on BottomSheetCoordinator so the
asynchronous offset update does not overwrite it, and re-enable the
test.

tabs: Start one insert animation per task, not one per inserted tab

12dac13b05de376a8d593232048f74203b080b5a
[ log ]

committer

Tue Nov 10 04:26:07 2026
tree
2d7cab862202f337c95a37af1d6a4d0e7f4d9383
parent
ad2ecba7981df4c6631338db73f357a8cc8fc476 [ diff ] 
tabs: Start one insert animation per task, not one per inserted tab

TabContainer::AddTabs() called StartInsertTabAnimation() for every
tab inserted into a visible strip, and that ended in
AnimateToIdealBounds(): a layout of the whole strip (UpdateIdealBounds)
followed by one AnimateViewTo() per tab and group header and a
PreferredSizeChanged(). TabStripModel reports one insertion per tab, so
when a session is restored, every tab inserted after the window has
been shown (which happens when the selected tab is restored) laid the
whole strip out again, each pass visiting every tab inserted so far.

Start the animation from a task posted at the first insert instead, so
that all tabs inserted within one task share a single pass. Within a
task the result is the same: BoundsAnimator never moves a view
synchronously, it records start and target bounds and moves the view
when the animation ticks, so the neighbour bounds that seed a new tab's
starting rectangle are unchanged whether or not an earlier insert in
the same task already started a pass, and one pass at the end of the
task produces the start/target pairs that a pass after every insert
would have left behind. While the pass is pending, IsAnimating()
reports true, so SetActiveTab() and Layout() take the animating branch
(no snap to the ideal bounds) and the preferred size keeps hugging the
current bounds, just as during the animation itself. The pending call
is held in a base::CancelableOnceClosure, which AnimateToIdealBounds()
and CancelAnimation(), and so CompleteAnimationAndLayout(), cancel if
they run first. Drag sessions keep starting the animation at once,
because the drag code reads the ideal bounds right after inserting
tabs. Tabs restored into a tab group still start a pass each, because
a change of group membership calls AnimateToIdealBounds() directly;
that is left as is here.

Code that reads the strip's animation state synchronously after an
insert runs the pending pass first, so that it sees what it saw when
the pass ran at insertion time: Layout(), whether the parent lays the
strip out later in the same task or a frame queued before the posted
task does. With rich animations off the pass is not deferred at all.
BoundsAnimator finishes a zero-duration animation as soon as it starts,
so there is nothing to coalesce: the strip was at rest before AddTabs()
returned, and its parent measures it before Layout() could run a
pending pass (CalculatePreferredSize() hugs the current bounds while
IsAnimating()), so a deferred pass would leave the strip sized for the
state before the insert. An insert that also changes the selection
still starts the pass at once: SetActiveTab() calls
AnimateToIdealBounds() when the selection change moves the ideal
bounds, i.e. once inactive tabs are narrower than the active one. Only
background inserts coalesce, which is what a session restore does.

Measured on a component build with DCHECKs on (Windows), session
restore of 1436 tabs into one window, two runs per variant:
  StartInsertTabAnimation() incl. the pass it started,
  summed over the inserted tabs:              0.91-0.96 s -> 0.01 s
  BrowserTabStripController::OnTabStripModelChanged()
  for the inserted tabs, total:               7.2-7.6 s -> 6.2-6.3 s
  TabStripModel observer notifications for
  the inserted tabs, total:                   9.0-9.5 s -> 7.9-8.1 s
  SessionRestore's per-tab restore, total:    28.3-30.3 s -> 26.8-27.7 s

On a release build (DCHECKs off) of the same tree, two runs per
variant, the pass is cheaper but still grows with the tab count:
  BrowserTabStripController::OnTabStripModelChanged()
  for the inserted tabs, total:               2.07-2.13 s -> 1.7-1.9 s
  (per tab, first to last 100 tabs:          1.5 -> 1.8 ms before,
                                              1.4 -> 1.2 ms after)
  TabStripModel observer notifications for
  the inserted tabs, total:                   2.59 s -> 2.2-2.4 s
  SessionRestore's per-tab restore, total:    6.2 s -> 5.9-6.6 s
                                              (within noise)

[Glic][Android] Fix TalkBack on the native FRE card

The FRE root is not important for accessibility, so View does not
promote it when it gets a pane title, and TalkBack may not announce
the title. Set the pane title on the content container instead.

passes touches through, but hover events from explore-by-touch and key
events still reached the card. For example, Enter on a focused close
button closed the sheet during the fade. Pass hover and key events
through too.

faa3b74211720b521c782045f8336c2ce2e68dc4
parent
9b116ad673ec457fd4c0a282207940ffe1f39b4c [ diff ] 
[TTC] Carry tool images in ToolResponse

Tools that produce something for the model to look at, such as
get_screenshot today and the planned get_pointer, have no way to return
an image. get_screenshot works around it by putting a base64 string in
the response JSON, which reaches the model as text rather than as an
image.

The TTC protocol already has a field for this,
ToolResponse.visual_content, but the client never set it. Add
VisualContent (encoded bytes plus MIME type) to ttc::ToolResponse, with
a Success() overload that takes one, and have TtcMesClient copy it into
the frame. The debug sink reports the image's MIME type and size.

No tool returns an image yet, so behavior is unchanged. Moving
get_screenshot over waits for the server to forward visual_content to
the model.

Design: go/ttc-get-pointer

Bug: b:571268295
Test: components_unittests --gtest_filter=TtcMesClientTest.*

Revert "Reland "media: Safe JNI ptr in MediaDrmStorageBridge""

This reverts commit 357f8a165df066e7a36ea6ae07eae307e659949d.

Reason for revert: Causes AfterClassException (LifetimeAssertException)
in batched instrumentation tests such as TabStripGroupContextMenuTest
(crbug.com/581157651). MediaDrmOriginIdManager starts background
Widevine pre-provisioning 1 minute after Profile startup via
MediaDrmBridge::CreateWithoutSessionSupport(), which creates a
MediaDrmStorageBridge JniRawPtrImpl and holds it alive on a background
sequence while waiting on ProvisionFetcher. When a @Batch(PER_CLASS)
test suite finishes @AfterClass while pre-provisioning is in flight,
LifetimeAssert.assertAllInstancesDestroyedForTesting() fails.

Original change's description:
> Reland "media: Safe JNI ptr in MediaDrmStorageBridge"
>
> This relands commit da9d4e061a6cc805cfed85a70056f39ec47faeb6
> (https://crrev.com/c/8700296), which was reverted in
> commit 986947eb4afc18471e51b7cb473f7c2f779f62bf
> (https://crrev.com/c/8712223).
>
> The original change only released the native MediaDrmStorageBridge
> JniRawPtr in MediaDrmBridge.destroy(). When MediaDrmBridge.create()
> returned null early (such as on emulators without Widevine support or
> when L1 security level setup failed) or tore down via
> MediaDrmBridge.release(), the JniRawPtr was left unreleased and
> triggered LifetimeAssertException on garbage collection.
>
> Ensure the JniRawPtr is always released by guarding
> MediaDrmBridge.create() with a try/finally block for early creation
> failures and releasing mStorage inside MediaDrmBridge.release().
>
> Original change's description:
> > media: Safe JNI ptr in MediaDrmStorageBridge
> >
> > Passing raw pointers between Java and C++ as long integer values is
> > unsafe and bypasses type-checking.
> >
> > Migrate MediaDrmStorageBridge to use JniRawPtr to manage native
> > references across the JNI boundary safely.
>
> Bug: 40794873, 581121764
> Include-Ci-Only-Tests: chromium.android:android-10-x86-rel|telemetry_perf_unittests_android_chrome
> Change-Id: I14199b88615fca31fe0d3594a7f4fcadc46b4043

[WebUI Bookmarks] Package bookmarks_resources.pak behind enable_webui_bookmarks

This CL conditions the compilation and packaging of WebUI Bookmarks
resources behind the `enable_webui_bookmarks` build flag and exposes
required shared WebUI dependencies to Android.

Specifically:
* chrome/browser/resources/BUILD.gn: Move `bookmarks:resources` from
  the desktop-only block into an `if (enable_webui_bookmarks)` block.
* chrome/browser/resources/bookmarks/BUILD.gn: Assert
  `enable_webui_bookmarks`.
* chrome/chrome_paks.gni: Condition `bookmarks_resources.pak` inclusion
  on `enable_webui_bookmarks`.
* ui/webui/resources: Relocate, `cr_components/managed_footnote` and
  `cr_elements` (`find_shortcut_*`, `cr_toolbar_selection_overlay.*`)
   `!is_ios` so they'd be bundled with WebUI bookmark builds on Android.

Standard mobile phone APKs (`enable_webui_bookmarks = false`) incur no
regression as `bookmarks_resources.pak` is completely excluded from
packaging.

92cd2062459b1b892141d0fb7dbf3ddad4f7eae4 [ diff ] 
MediaRouter: Use JniRawPtr in DialogController

Passing native pointers as raw long values bypasses MiraclePtr
protections and type safety across the JNI boundary.

Migrate BrowserMediaRouterDialogController to hold a JniRawPtr to
MediaRouterDialogControllerAndroid. The C++ object is owned as
WebContentsUserData and lives with the WebContents, while Java borrows
the pointer across dialog requests. The pointer is cleared when the
native controller is destroyed.

PdfInkSignatureCreationHandler and reply with mesh coat outline
vertices:

  "finishSignatureStrokeReply" messages to the client.

  rendering them as filled Skia paths and comparing against PNG
  baselines with MatchesPngFile().

Tue Nov 10 03:51:42 2026
tree
ff59740d4ddc72a1833ddaecdbf92377672e4bfc
parent
cc1c9820f0c8e7ef29eb9e144fd2b8c0b696024c [ diff ] 
[media] Spanify Vp8BoolDecoder

Vp8BoolDecoder tracked the coded stream with three raw_ptrs (start,
current position and end) and advanced the current one with pointer
arithmetic under UNSAFE_TODO. Keep the unread part of the stream in a
base::raw_span instead and pop bytes off its front with
take_first_elem(), so every byte FillDecoder() loads is bounds-checked
and BitOffset() no longer subtracts pointers.

Initialize() now takes a base::span, which resolves the TODO on it.
Its only caller, Vp8Parser::ParseFrameHeader(), already holds the
frame as a span. The new member keeps DanglingUntriaged; removing it
is tracked separately in crrev.com/c/8299273.

No intended behavior change. Adds tests for empty input and for
reading past the end of the stream.

R=tguilbert@chromium.org

ContextMenu: Drop native ptr in ContextMenuHelper

ContextMenuHelper previously stored its C++ peer address in a raw long
field both to key its static instance map and to notify native when a
context menu closed.

Key the static instance map by a monotonic Java peer ID stored on C++
ContextMenuHelper, and resolve ContextMenuHelper::FromWebContents in
onContextMenuClosed from mWebContents so Java stores no native pointer.

f6f00f863452ee398c350126dafafa03a3d53024 [ diff ] 
WebView: Use JniRawPtr in AwSettings

AwSettings previously stored its WebContents-owned native peer as a raw
long, bypassing compile-time type safety and BackupRefPtr quarantine
tracking across the JNI boundary.

Replace the raw long handle with JniRawPtr<NativeAwSettings>, register
it with AwContents.registerSafePointer() so the handle is released if
AwContents is garbage collected before native teardown, and unregister,
release, and clear mNativeAwSettings under mAwSettingsLock when the
native peer is destroyed.

031d6914c315c84b7f54d5e25016ca45b7c0b768 [ diff ] 
ash: Skip zero-distance shelf bounds animations

BoundsAnimator::AnimateViewTo runs a full-duration slide animation even
when a view is already at its target bounds. Calling
ShelfView::AnimateToIdealBounds when no shelf items or separator moved
therefore kept ShelfView::IsAnimating true for 300 ms, delaying
post-login shelf animation completion and BootTime.Login3 by ~180 ms.

Skip item and separator bounds animations when already at target bounds
and fade in newly added items directly when no bounds animation runs.

Content: Use JniRawPtr in ScreenCapture

ScreenCapture previously held its C++ content::DesktopCapturerAndroid
peer in a volatile raw long field across JNI.

Store the borrowed reference in a volatile
JniRawPtr<NativeDesktopCapturerAndroid> created via jni_zero::MakeRaw so
BackupRefPtr quarantines the C++ referent until destroy() releases the
handle on the desktop capturer sequence, and pass the pointer to
@NativeMethods callbacks via JniPtr<NativeDesktopCapturerAndroid>.

tree
09793210d929e4af4b0b54b9b4b1e69ff7b7a744
parent
8fd0b1bc04c1ede29a418dddf8e576d21f2f9718 [ diff ] 
Linux: Read lower-precedence policies from $SNAP_DATA/policies in snaps

Canonical's Chromium snap carries snap-policies-path.patch, which
replaces chrome::DIR_POLICY_FILES with $SNAP_DATA/policies when
SNAP_DATA is set. That can't be upstreamed as is: SNAP_DATA is an
environment variable, so on a regular deb or rpm install any user could
bypass enterprise policy (and the enrollment token, which is also read
from DIR_POLICY_FILES) with `SNAP_DATA=/tmp/x google-chrome`.

The standard policy directory already works in a snap through the
browser-support interface. $SNAP_DATA/policies is still useful where
/etc/opt isn't writable (Ubuntu Core) and for per-snap policies, so add
it as an additional, lower-precedence source of platform policy:

- ConfigDirPolicyLoader takes a vector<ConfigDirectory> in decreasing
  order of precedence, each with the standard managed/ and recommended/
  layout and an optional OwnershipRequirement. Earlier directories win
  conflicts at the same level. This replaces the single-directory
  constructor; the only other caller (remoting's PolicyWatcher) is
  updated.
- When an OwnershipRequirement is set on a ConfigDirectory, a policy
  file in it is only read if the file and every directory from
  trusted_base_dir down to it are owned by trusted_uid, aren't symlinks
  and aren't writable by group or others. Symlinks in the configured
  path are resolved first, since $SNAP_DATA is /var/snap/<name>/current,
  a symlink to the current revision. This ownership check, not the
  environment, is the security boundary: an unprivileged user can't
  create a root-controlled directory under `/`. The check is only
  available on Linux and Mac, so OwnershipRequirement must not be used
  elsewhere.
- ChromeBrowserPolicyConnector appends $SNAP_DATA/policies (requiring
  root ownership up to `/`) when GetAppSandbox() is kSnap.
- base::VerifyPathControlledByUser() was Mac-only; enable it on Linux
  too for the ownership check.

chrome::DIR_POLICY_FILES and the enrollment token location are
unchanged.

6bb3dd2a70db8760a344d1c7f71aee4d70f056d1 [ diff ] 
Revert scroll_event_sent_ CHECK in GestureProvider::OnFling

In certain gesture flows, scroll_event_sent_ can be false when OnFling
is called, which was previously handled gracefully by the fallback logic
in if (!scroll_event_sent_). Replacing the CHECK with a DCHECK prevents
crashes during gesture handling while preserving debug assertion
coverage.

This fix was generated by the Crash Autofix pipeline for crash report
crash/15a70f0088400000. Please see go/crash-autofix for more details.

4f37115b35ddc88a83118ca9eb519ff917aa6e9d
parent
b822e5a094be96a61590ecc1a3b6a3c98b33b5f7 [ diff ] 
[a11y] Test hidden-via-style for text without a LayoutObject

Text nodes in display:none content, <rp>, or canvas fallback have no
LayoutObject and no computed style of their own. They were once always
reported as not hidden via style. That was fixed by inheriting the
hidden state from the parent (crrev.com/c/4002849), which is covered
only indirectly by DumpAccessibilityTree baselines, and a stale TODO for
this bug remained.

Add unit tests covering layoutless text in display:none, <rp>, canvas
fallback (both hidden and visible), visibility:hidden, and a dynamic
display:none toggle. Removing the parent-inheritance block makes four of
them fail.

Replace the stale TODO in ComputeIsHiddenViaStyle() with a comment
explaining which objects reach the layoutless-style branch, and use the
equivalent GetElement() check. No behavior change.

86a4571ad65fe7df5c1d51e69a179be94272fc59
parent
55e3b942a13cd1336efba7c5642123f16bd90101 [ diff ] 
WebView: Own AwDrawFnImpl via JniUniquePtr

AwDrawFnImpl previously stored its native handle as a raw long,
bypassing compile-time type safety and LifetimeAssert lifecycle checks
across the JNI boundary.

Replace the raw long field with JniUniquePtr<NativeAwDrawFnImpl> and
specialize jni_zero::TemplatedDeleter<AwDrawFnImpl> to release the
functor handle via ReleaseHandle() instead of calling operator delete
directly, since libhwui retains its own reference until its release
callback fires.

Ensure every functor is destroyed through onDetachedFromWindow() by
calling mPrimaryAwViewMethods.onDetachedFromWindow() in
AwContents.destroy() (so fullscreen teardown is not skipped by
NullAwViewMethods) and gating hardware draw in onDrawInner() on
mIsAttachedToWindow, matching native AwContents::OnDraw.

67577bd0c6739bbc524e1f560d42a7a327af0eb5
parent
da8eedc2f486b7a8fa4fb73667babb2dbe759ea0 [ diff ] 
Content: Own CaptioningController in WebContents

CaptioningController previously allocated a self-owned native peer and
stored its address in a raw long field, relying on Java UserData.destroy
to delete the native object. In WebView, AwContents clears its
WebContentsInternals reference before native WebContents teardown, so
Java UserData.destroy never runs and the native CaptioningController
leaked on every WebView where accessibility was initialized.

Make CaptioningController a WebContentsUserData<CaptioningController> so
WebContents owns and destroys it directly on native teardown, and route
setTextTrackSettings through WebContents instead of storing a raw native
pointer in Java.

48e304bb10f00fff93d6f647ebe70d7f54c73ee2 [ diff ] 
WebView: Own AwPermissionRequest via JniUniquePtr

AwPermissionRequest previously transferred ownership of its native peer
to Java as a raw long and invoked a manual destroy JNI method from its
CleanupReference, bypassing compile-time type safety and LifetimeAssert
lifecycle checks across the JNI boundary.

Pass ownership from AwPermissionRequest::Create via jni_zero::MakeUnique
into JniUniquePtr<NativeAwPermissionRequest>, destroy the owned handle
from CleanupReference on explicit grant/deny/cancel or garbage
collection, and remove the manual Destroy() JNI method.

accessibility attributes on macOS Privacy Overlay backdrop windows:

[loomnibox] Rename is_pending_screenshot to is_pending_context_upload

Generalize `is_pending_screenshot` to `is_pending_context_upload` (and
`screenshot_origin_was_searchbox_` to
`context_upload_origin_was_searchbox_`) across Mojo, C++, and WebUI in
preparation for reusing the collapsed pending-upload Composebox state
when the native file chooser is open.

This CL is a pure mechanical rename with no behavioral changes that is
used in a follow up CL to fix the file picker sheet from jumping.

parent
a0322ec4ea4d5cc65ec1430f20ae3d4f1f8ec76b [ diff ] 
[Themes] Match tab group dialog colors with tab strip in custom themes

This CL updates `kColorTabGroupContextMenu*` (and its alias
`kColorTabGroupDialog*`) in `AddChromeColorMixer()` to alias
`kColorTabGroupTabStripFrameActive*` directly instead of calling
`SelectColorBasedOnDarkInputOrMode(dark_mode, ...)`.

Previously, `SelectColorBasedOnDarkInputOrMode()` unconditionally chose
`params.base_dark` whenever `dark_mode` was true, ignoring custom theme
frame colors. When a light custom or autogenerated theme was active in
dark mode, the tab group header chip on the tab strip selected the dark
shade (`params.base_light`) to contrast against the light tab strip,
while the tab group editor bubble color picker circles selected the
light shade (`params.base_dark`), causing the Grey color option to
appear white in the picker while rendering dark grey on the tab strip.

Removing `SelectColorBasedOnDarkInputOrMode()` and aliasing to the
active tab strip frame color ensures tab group dialog and context menu
color dots match the active tab strip header chip across custom themes.

326e03b60ded1ed49a429c2aa9bbec10ad778254
parent
cb0f0442d5c7edd26ad7b4e97fd1b51eee3ad35f [ diff ] 
[iOS] Make `OverrideGetHostedDomainImplementation()` scoped

The impact of `OverrideGetHostedDomainImplementation()` is not
automatically reset at the end of a test. Tests had to reset it
explicitly, and sometimes forgot to do so.

This CL thus introduces a scoped value that does the reset when the
value is destructed, ensuring the problem can’t occur again. This also
allowed to find and correct tests that did not clean their side effect
as they should have.

Setting an override while another one is active is now a `CHECK()`
failure, so that destroying a nested override does not remove the outer
one.

parent
17ed51e49634f34fd5a3a662b83cbf0d05a4b63f [ diff ] 
[CodeHealth] Remove Pepper OpenDevice and CloseDevice from MediaStream

`MediaStreamDispatcherHost::OpenDevice()` and `CloseDevice()` were the
Mojo entry points of `PepperMediaDeviceManager`, which was removed in
crrev.com/c/6664918. No renderer code calls them anymore, but the
browser still carries the whole path: `MediaStreamManager::OpenDevice()`
and `OpenDeviceRequest`, the `MEDIA_OPEN_DEVICE_PEPPER_ONLY` request
type, and its special cases in `MediaStreamManager` and
`MediaStreamDevicesController`.

This CL removes the two Mojo methods and the Pepper-only request type,
along with the tests that exercised them.

`VideoCaptureTest` and the `VideoCaptureHost` MojoLPM fuzzer used
`MediaStreamManager::OpenDevice()` as a shortcut to open a video capture
session. They now go through `GenerateStreams()`, like getUserMedia
does. `MediaStreamDispatcherHostTest.Salt` does the same, since the salt
invalidation it covers isn't specific to Pepper.

f9dffd5c22a46e70acb87e1eeb527057f059a533
parent
0a8211bfd18849002591cd753a413d44c72c8aea [ diff ] 
[WebNN] Show requested device and EP details in webnn-internals

webnn-internals did not show which device type a context requested, so a
request that ended up elsewhere (e.g. NPU requested but WebGPU selected)
was hard to notice. It also did not show which library each EP was
loaded from, which is easy to lose track of when testing local EP builds
via --webnn-ort-ep-library-path-for-testing, nor the driver version that
the NPU driver blocklist checks.

Context cards now show the requested device. Each EP additionally shows
its library path and OS driver version, read from the "library_path" and
"os_driver_version" EP metadata, on both the context cards and the info
page. The existing "Version" label is renamed to "EP Version" to make
clear it is the EP's own version, not the driver's.

The new mojom fields (library_path, driver_version and
requested_device_type) are used only for introspection. They flow from
the GPU process to the browser and the WebUI, never to the renderer.

PageImage: Use JniUniquePtr in ImageServiceBridge

Replace the raw long mNativeImageServiceBridge field in
ImageServiceBridge with JniUniquePtr so JNI Zero tracks native peer
lifetime for ::ImageServiceBridge and prevents use-after-free across JNI
calls.

Return jni_zero::MakeUnique from the C++ initializer, invoke
JniUniquePtr.destroy() and clear mNativeImageServiceBridge in destroy(),
and guard fetchImageUrlFor() when the handle is null.

c30969ba94337a57ebac1d2d22f55a4ea2bfdf6b
parent
4ce8cac65980dc573a1933b5eecae3b799b672ef [ diff ] 
TTS: Clear TtsEngine native pointer on destroy

TtsPlatformImpl.TtsEngine holds its own copy of the native
TtsPlatformImplAndroid pointer. Previously, TtsPlatformImpl.destroy()
only zeroed the outer field while leaving the default TtsEngine copy
non-zero, and TtsEngine.initializeDefault() read the field on a
background worker thread before calling voicesChanged() in
onPostExecute() without checking whether native teardown had occurred.

Propagate native teardown to the default TtsEngine so asynchronous
initialization skips voicesChanged() once the C++ peer is destroyed,
and drop the background-thread read of the UI-thread pointer field.

tree
f20750a21fc4ed20b44792ae3281fdf3f336ef05
parent
061990b8c4854064fd6f069324ea3bab20365507 [ diff ] 
[X11] Notify display observers when only the primary display changes

Switching the primary output with e.g. `xrandr --output X --primary`
changes neither bounds nor work area, so
DisplayChangeNotifier::NotifyDisplaysChanged() sent no observer
callback. XDisplayManager did pick up the new primary, but observers
that cache it, such as DesktopMessagePopupCollection (message center
notification popups), kept using the old primary display.

Pass the old and new primary display ids to NotifyDisplaysChanged()
and report DISPLAY_METRIC_PRIMARY for the display that became primary,
matching what DisplayList (used by Ozone/Wayland) does. ScreenWin is
unchanged.

tree
bf08ea5b49a4c84d5c74dd3697055a52b785dd05
parent
4788e2436f22b7ff2141a11fd33a7036eb51ea70 [ diff ] 
[i18n] Add scoped test locales for multi-locale tests

There is currently many places where chrome is not properly formatted.
Lists where ", " is hard coded as separator. Numbers that are displayed
as "1,234,567.8" and not localized (so French user don’t see
"1.234.567,8" as they should). Percent numbers hard coded as "NNN%"
(while French users should see "NNN %"). Many issues that can’t just be
fixed as part of GRD translation process as translators don’t see those
strings in the first place.

While we don’t usually test i18n outside of the i18n library, I believe
in this case it’s important to add regression tests for those issues.
Firstly because this will allow to catch regression. But also to have
examples showing agents that we should not hard code English syntax.

To test code in another locale, instantiate
`ui::test::ScopedApplicationLocale` with a `base::i18n::LanguageTag`
within a test scope. It combines `base::i18n::ScopedDefaultIcuLocale`
(ICU and `base::i18n` default locale) with the new
`ui::test::ScopedResourceBundleLocale` (`ui::ResourceBundle` localized
strings), overrides `LANGUAGE` on Linux with GLib, resets the cached
`base` number formatters, and restores the original state on
destruction. Both classes live in the test-only
`//ui/base/l10n/test:test_support` target and `CHECK_IS_TEST()`.
`ui::test::ScopedResourceBundleLocale` passes the locale to
`ui::ResourceBundle` as a string until it receives a
`base::i18n::LanguageTag` (https://crbug.com/582341363).

Because `ui_base_unittests` only bundles `en-US.pak`, the tests of
`ui::ResourceBundle` reloading across locales are placed in
`chrome/browser/ui/scoped_application_locale_unittest.cc` (`unit_tests`,
which bundles all localized `.pak` files via
`//chrome:packed_resources`).

`L10nUtilTest.ScopedApplicationLocaleFormatsLists` in
`ui_base_unittests` includes Japanese, Thai and Arabic, which do not
separate list items with ", ", to show that the whole list pattern is
localized, and not only the word "and".

[Glic][Android] Crossfade the native FRE card out on accept

Start chat now fades the native FRE card out over 380ms instead of
removing it at once, so the WebUI behind it shows through.
GlicBottomSheetFreCoordinator.fadeOut() runs the fade with
GlicBottomSheetFreTransitionController, and destroy() cancels it. The
fade always runs; it does not wait for WebUI load state. The card stops
taking touches when the fade starts. Chrome is told about the accept
right away; the card is removed when the fade ends, or at once if the
sheet content is torn down.

[Linux Wayland] Fix window shrinking on compositor-driven unmaximize
📋 Metadata
commit
c2f31a06a5794158676fe487d00d715e3d4c0579
[ log ]
author

Tue Nov 10 02:16:40 2026
committer

Tue Nov 10 02:16:40 2026
tree
cc4f71293dc751bf418c587d004dcacc4a4ddc25
parent
0cbe7bc8cab2f66510255aca1cc52e7af31e684e [ diff ] 
[Linux Wayland] Fix window shrinking on compositor-driven unmaximize

Since [1],
BrowserFrameViewLayoutLinuxNative::RestoredFrameBorderInsets() gets the
GTK frame provider for the current maximized state, and GetInputInsets()
returns no insets while the frame is condensed. When the compositor
unmaximizes the window (e.g. Super+Down on GNOME, or dragging the
titlebar of a maximized window), the configure size is converted to
bounds while the window is still maximized, so the decoration insets
are not added. The window geometry set afterwards includes the insets
and gets clamped to the smaller buffer by the compositor, so the window
shrinks by the shadow size on every maximize/restore cycle.

This is the same issue that was fixed for BrowserFrameViewLayoutLinux in
[2]. Always use the restored frame provider and restored input insets
when computing the restored frame border insets.

[1] https://chromium-review.googlesource.com/c/chromium/src/+/6276531
[2] https://chromium-review.googlesource.com/c/chromium/src/+/5739096

Fixed: 353304832

parent
e875c9f16f25442fd3302e654bbcde5d9be3cca2 [ diff ] 
[WebUI] Remove --user-color-source from ThemeSource::GenerateColorsCss()

Once devtools-frontend reads userColorSource directly from
DevToolsUIBindings (HostConfig and DevToolsAPI.colorThemeChanged), no
WebUI or DevTools consumer reads the --user-color-source CSS custom
property from generated colors.css stylesheets.

Remove the is_grayscale and is_baseline parameters and the appended
--user-color-source declarations from ThemeSource::GenerateColorsCss(),
making generated colors.css purely a function of ui::ColorProvider and
request query parameters. Also drop the corresponding ThemeService
lookups from ThemeSource::SendColorsCss() and
ThemeColorsSourceManager::PopulateLocalResourceLoaderConfig(..., const
ui::ColorProvider&).

Bug: 41495790, 40280130
